hidden"><span class="type-sans-body7 shrink-0 transition-colors align-middle text-mid-contrast line-clamp-2 overflow-hidden text-ellipsis" aria-current="page">The EU’s Response to SolarWinds</span></li></ol></nav><div class="flex items-center gap-3 md:gap-6 ml-auto"><button type="button" class="gap-1 text-high-contrast hover:text-accent inline-flex items-center" data-topper="article"><div class="contents col-span-6 row-span-3 row-start-1 md:block lg:col-span-10"><div class="col-span-full md:col-span-6 lg:col-span-10"><div class="mb-4 flex flex-wrap gap-2 lg:mb-6"><a class="type-mono-ui4 px-1.5 py-1 whitespace-nowrap transition-colors w-fit border border-low-contrast text-high-contrast hover:bg-high-contrast hover:text-base hover:border-high-contrast" href="/topics/cybersecurity">Cybersecurity</a></div></div><div class="col-span-full md:col-span-6 md:row-start-2 lg:col-span-10"><h1 class="type-serif-h2 italic">The EU’s Response to SolarWinds</h1></div><div class="col-span-full rich-text type-sans-body4 md:col-span-6 lg:col-span-10 mt-4 md:row-start-3 md:mt-6 lg:mt-12"><p>While EU’s issuance of the declaration of solidarity in response to the SolarWinds cyber campaign is a sign of progress, it fails to provide clarification on what, if any, further actions can be expected from Brussels.</p>
</div></div><div class="col-span-full aspect-16/9 print:hidden mt-8 md:col-span-6 md:row-span-3 md:row-start-1 md:mt-0 lg:col-span-12 lg:col-start-13"><figure class="@container"><img class="object-cover aspect-3/2 w-full overflow-hidden" src="https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_1920/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg" srcSet="https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_320/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 320w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_480/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 480w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_640/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 640w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_768/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 768w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_1024/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 1024w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_1280/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 1280w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_1440/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 1440w, https://assets.cfr.org/images/t_cfr_3_2/f_auto/w_1920/v1758143961/rtxc11r3/rtxc11r3.jpg 1920w" sizes="100vw" alt="&lt;p&gt;European Union flags flutter outside the EU Commission headquarters in Brussels, Belgium.&lt;/p&gt;

</span> <span class="text-mid-contrast">REUTERS/Francois Lenoir/File Photo</span></figcaption></figure></div></header></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid border-t border-low-contrast lg:grid lg:grid-cols-site lg:gap-x-site-grid-gutter"><h2 class="sr-only" id="_R_a7av5ubtlb_">By experts and staff</h2><div class="col-span-12 size-fit w-full pt-3 a.m.</time></dd></div></dl></div><div class="col-span-12 flex flex-col gap-12 print:gap-2"><div class="flex flex-col pt-3 max-lg:border-t max-lg:border-low-contrast"><ul class="grid items-start 4.45471 25.2866C7.13847 25.2866 8.53376 23.4647 9.14714 22.5295L8.65993 22.238C8.34496 22.718 7.13847 24.2552 5.44109 24.2552C3.93159 24.2552 3.14968 23.1419 3.14968 21.2066C3.14968 17.9638 4.92626 xmlns="http://www.w3.org/2000/svg" class="size-4" aria-hidden="true"><g><path d="M5.90723 8.09375L8.75098 5.25" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.25" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path><path d="M12.2332 2.30714C12.2543 2.2323 12.255 2.1532 12.2354 2.07797C12.2158 2.00274 12.1764 1.9341 12.1215 1.87912C12.0665 1.82414 11.9978 1.7848 11.9226 1.76516C11.8474 1.74552 11.7683 1.74629 11.6934 1.76738L1.19343 4.95128C1.1077 4.97548 1.0314 5.02526 0.974733 5.09398C0.91806 5.16269 0.883713 5.24707 0.876282 5.33583C0.86885 5.4246 0.888689 5.51351 0.933145 5.5907C0.977602 5.66788 1.04456 5.72966 1.12507 5.76777L5.90639 8.09417L8.2328 12.875C8.27091 12.9555 8.33269 13.0224 8.40987 13.0669C8.48706 13.1113 8.57597 13.1312 8.66473 13.1237C8.7535 13.1163 8.83787 13.082 8.90659 13.0253C8.97531 12.9686 9.02509 12.8923 9.04929 12.8066L12.2332 2.30714Z" stroke="currentColor" stroke-width="1.25" stroke-linecap="round" stroke-linejoin="round"></path></g></svg></button></div></div></div><div id="page-content" data-testid="page-content" class="space-y-article-blocks mbs-8 gap-y-site-grid-gutter relative w-full md:mt-16"><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span><em>Julia Schuetze is the Jr Project Director for International Cybersecurity Policy at Stiftung Neue Verantwortung e.V. </em><em>Arthur de Liedekerke is a cybersecurity analyst with prior experience in the European Union institutions. </em><em>The views and opinions expressed in this article are solely those of the authors and do not reflect the official position or policy of their employers.</em></span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>On April 15, the same day that the United States <a href="https://www.whitehouse.gov/briefing-room/statements-releases/2021/04/15/fact-sheet-imposing-costs-for-harmful-foreign-activities-by-the-russian-government/" target="_blank" title="imposed" rel="noopener noreferrer">imposed</a> sanctions against Russia for the SolarWinds cyber espionage campaign, election interference, and other issues, the European Union (EU) <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/" target="_blank" title="published" rel="noopener noreferrer">published</a> a declaration expressing solidarity with the United States. A month later, on May 14, the European Council <a href="https://protect-us.mimecast.com/s/rEbtCmZMqQS5PKWouGYoUB?domain=consilium.europa.eu/" target="_blank" title="extended" rel="noopener noreferrer">extended</a> the sanctions regime that guides response to cyber attacks that threaten the EU or its members until May 2022. As part of the EU’s cyber diplomacy toolbox, a declaration falls into <a href="https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-13007-2017-INIT/en/pdf" target="_blank" title="category three" rel="noopener noreferrer">category three</a> [PDF] of responses—stability measures—which express concern or condemn general cyber trends or certain cyber activities and could have a signaling function. While the EU’s issuance of the declaration in response to SolarWinds is a sign of progress, it fails to provide clarification on what, if any, further actions can be expected from Brussels.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>The declaration still matters because it shows that the EU’s response process to malicious cyber activities is maturing. This declaration differs from previous responses due to its coordinated timing with the imposition of U.S. sanctions and reference to Washington’s attribution of the campaign to Russia. This contrasts with previous EU responses where <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2018/04/16/malicious-cyber-activities-council-adopts-conclusions/" target="_blank" title="attribution was not mentioned explicitly" rel="noopener noreferrer">attribution was not mentioned explicitly</a> or where member states independently <a href="https://www.zdnet.com/article/blaming-russia-for-notpetya-was-coordinated-diplomatic-action/" target="_blank" title="coordinated their attribution efforts multilaterally" rel="noopener noreferrer">coordinated their attribution efforts multilaterally</a> with third states, avoiding attribution through a unified <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2018/04/16/malicious-cyber-activities-council-adopts-conclusions/" target="_blank" title="EU-wide response" rel="noopener noreferrer">EU-wide response</a>.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>Additionally, the language of the EU’s recent declaration deviates from prior statements, like those responding to <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2020/02/21/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-call-to-promote-and-conduct-responsible-behaviour-in-cyberspace/" target="_blank" title="election interference" rel="noopener noreferrer">election interference</a> in Georgia and cyber threats <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2020/04/30/declaration-by-the-high-representative-josep-borrell-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-on-malicious-cyber-activities-exploiting-the-coronavirus-pandemic/" target="_blank" title="targeting" rel="noopener noreferrer">targeting</a> the health-care sector during the pandemic. Instead of “condemning” the SolarWinds campaign, the declaration expressed the EU’s solidarity with the United States “<a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/" target="_blank" title="on the impact of malicious cyber activities" rel="noopener noreferrer">on the impact of malicious cyber activities</a><a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/" target="_blank" title="." rel="noopener noreferrer"><em>.</em></a>”<em> </em>Simply expressing solidarity with the impact in the United States reflects that the European Union does not per se condemn this type of activity. Moreover, it treads carefully when referring to the impact the operation had on the EU and its member states.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>This could have been more directly connected to where the EU explains its concern about malicious activities in general that are “affecting the security and integrity of information and communication technology (ICT) products and services, which might have systemic effects and cause significant harm to our society, security and economy.” This vague description could be an attempt to show that, from an EU standpoint, the seemingly indiscriminate nature of the SolarWinds breach (“the compromise affected governments and businesses worldwide, including in EU Member States”) and apparent disregard for collateral damage (“systemic effects with potential significant harm to our society, security and economy”) could result in consequences for the perpetrator. However, by not being more concrete on the damage the campaign had on the EU, falls short of indicating whether those behind it crossed a red line that requires a strong response from the EU. As the rest of the statement focuses on preventative efforts that the EU is already undertaking, the text does not signal that such incidents could be met with a more assertive response, such as restrictive measures. The statement also ignores the fact that two days before the Declaration came out  technical evidence seems to suggest that the EU’s sanctions regime could be applied. In an <a href="https://www.europarl.europa.eu/doceo/document/P-9-2021-001112-ASW_EN.pdf" target="_blank" title="answer to a parliamentary question" rel="noopener noreferrer">answer to a parliamentary question</a> on April 13, European Commissioner for Budget and Administration Johannes Hahn indicated that the EU’s computer emergency response team (CERT-EU) had identified cases where IT networks and systems had been<b> “</b>significantly impacted” and personal data breaches occurred. “Significant impact” or “potentially significant effect” is one threshold <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2019/05/17/cyber-attacks-council-is-now-able-to-impose-sanctions/" target="_blank" title="covered in the sanctions regime" rel="noopener noreferrer">covered in the sanctions regime</a>.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>Whether the EU will follow up <a href="https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-5664-2018-INIT/en/pdf" target="_blank" title="with sanctions" rel="noopener noreferrer">with sanctions</a>—like the United States—will also likely depend on two things: the collective determination to impose costs despite the risks of retaliation, and on the outcome of further investigation mentioned in the Declaration itself vis-à-vis the other required thresholds for sanctions..</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>Member states appear ready to act. Unofficial reports indicate that a number of <a href="https://tass.com/world/1278807" target="_blank" title="EU member states are toying with the idea of introducing sanctions" rel="noopener noreferrer">EU member states are toying with the idea of introducing sanctions</a> against Russian citizens who were allegedly involved in the SolarWinds campaign. Also, given the steady deterioration of EU-Russia relations in recent months, member states could be tempted to demonstrate their collective determination to push back against Russia and their commitment to the transatlantic alliance.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>With the second issue, it’s possible that sanctions have not yet been imposed by the EU because its investigation is ongoing. Historically, EU cyber sanctions have been <a href="https://www.sueddeutsche.de/politik/hack-bundestag-angriff-russland-1.4891668" target="_blank" title="imposed" rel="noopener noreferrer">imposed</a> against Russian individuals and entities only after the collection of evidence and attribution by at least two EU member states. Attribution by the United States could be insufficient on its own to warrant such a response. It is also possible that the EU is taking its time to aiming to create a sanctions bundle. Like the United States, the EU has <a href="https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2020/07/30/eu-imposes-the-first-ever-sanctions-against-cyber-attacks/" target="_blank" title="sanctioned different entities for various malicious activities at the same time." rel="noopener noreferrer">sanctioned different entities for various malicious activities at the same time.</a></span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span>The EU response process is maturing, showing steady improvement in its coordination with third countries. Still, it would have been better if the EU had explicitly described the impact the SolarWinds attack had on EU interests. Such clarity would, if Brussels does eventually follow through with a stronger response such as sanctions, signal that the attacks crossed a threshold. However, considering the time constraint of aligning with the U.S. response and the need for consensus-making in the EU, it is possible that the Declaration was only a first careful response and it will soon follow-up with stronger language and/ or other means.</span></p></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1" data-block="core/paragraph" data-block-start="core/paragraph" data-block-end="core/paragraph" data-block-align=""><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"><p class="rich-text type-serif-body4"><span> </span></p></div></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1"><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 col-span-full sm:col-span-8 lg:col-start-13 lg:col-end-25"></div></div><div class="grid-cols-site gap-x-site-grid-gutter px-site-margin grid grid-rows-1 mt-8"><div class="lg:row-start-1 lg:row-end-1 April 15, the same day that the United States \u003ca href=\"https://www.whitehouse.gov/briefing-room/statements-releases/2021/04/15/fact-sheet-imposing-costs-for-harmful-foreign-activities-by-the-russian-government/\" target=\"_blank\" href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/\" A month later, on May 14, the European Council \u003ca href=\"https://protect-us.mimecast.com/s/rEbtCmZMqQS5PKWouGYoUB?domain=consilium.europa.eu/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"extended\"\u003eextended\u003c/a\u003e the sanctions regime that guides response to cyber attacks that threaten the EU or its members until May 2022. As part of the EU’s cyber diplomacy toolbox, a declaration falls into \u003ca href=\"https://data.consilium.europa.eu/doc/document/ST-13007-2017-INIT/en/pdf\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"category three\"\u003ecategory three\u003c/a\u003e [PDF] of responses—stability measures—which express concern or condemn general cyber trends or certain cyber activities and could have a signaling function. While the EU’s issuance of the declaration in response to SolarWinds is a sign of progress, it fails to provide clarification on what, if any, further actions can be expected from Brussels.55:T467,The declaration still matters because it shows that the EU’s response process to malicious cyber activities is maturing. This declaration differs from previous responses due to its coordinated timing with the imposition of U.S. sanctions and reference to Washington’s attribution of the campaign to Russia. This contrasts with previous EU responses where \u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2018/04/16/malicious-cyber-activities-council-adopts-conclusions/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"attribution was not mentioned explicitly\"\u003eattribution was not mentioned explicitly\u003c/a\u003e or where member states independently \u003ca href=\"https://www.zdnet.com/article/blaming-russia-for-notpetya-was-coordinated-diplomatic-action/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"coordinated their attribution efforts multilaterally\"\u003ecoordinated their attribution efforts multilaterally\u003c/a\u003e with third states, avoiding attribution through a unified \u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2018/04/16/malicious-cyber-activities-council-adopts-conclusions/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"EU-wide response\"\u003eEU-wide hidden print:block\",\"children\":[\"$\",\"p\",null,{\"children\":\"Council on Foreign Relations\"}]}]\n65:I[198975,[\"$4\",\"$5\",\"$6\",\"$7\",\"$8\",\"$9\",\"$a\",\"$b\",\"$c\",\"$d\",\"$e\",\"$f\"],\"SearchModal\"]\n63:T6fa,Additionally, the language of the EU’s recent declaration deviates from prior statements, like those responding to \u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2020/02/21/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-call-to-promote-and-conduct-responsible-behaviour-in-cyberspace/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"election interference\"\u003eelection interference\u003c/a\u003e in Georgia and cyber threats \u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2020/04/30/declaration-by-the-high-representative-josep-borrell-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-on-malicious-cyber-activities-exploiting-the-coronavirus-pandemic/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"targeting\"\u003etargeting\u003c/a\u003e the health-care sector during the pandemic. Instead of “condemning” the SolarWinds campaign, the declaration expressed the EU’s solidarity with the United States “\u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"on the impact of malicious cyber activities\"\u003eon the impact of malicious cyber activities\u003c/a\u003e\u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2021/04/15/declaration-by-the-high-representative-on-behalf-of-the-european-union-expressing-solidarity-with-the-united-states-on-the-impact-of-the-solarwinds-cyber-operation/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\".\"\u003e\u003cem\u003e.\u003c/em\u003e\u003c/a\u003e”\u003cem\u003e \u003c/em\u003eSimply expressing solidarity with the impact in the United States reflects that the European Union does not per se condemn this type of activity. Moreover, it treads carefully when referring to the impact the operation had on the EU and its member states.56:[\"$\",\"$L52\",\"3\",{\"align\":\"\",\"data-block\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-start\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-end\":\"core/paragraph\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L53\",\"0\",{\"block\":{\"name\":\"core/paragraph\",\"attributes\":{\"content\":\"$63\",\"dropCap\":false}}}]]}]\n64:T854,This could have been more directly connected to where the EU explains its concern about malicious activities in general that are “affecting the security and integrity of information and communication technology (ICT) products and services, which might have systemic effects and cause significant harm to our society, security and economy.” This vague description could be an attempt to show that, from an EU standpoint, the seemingly indiscriminate nature of the SolarWinds breach (“the compromise affected governments and businesses worldwide, including in EU Member States”) and apparent disregard for collateral damage (“systemic effects with potential significant harm to our society, security and economy”) could result in consequences for the perpetrator. However, by not being more concrete on the damage the campaign had on the EU, falls short of indicating whether those behind it crossed a red line that requires a strong response from the EU. As the rest of the statement focuses on preventative efforts that the EU is already undertaking, the text does not signal that such incidents could be met with a more assertive response, such as restrictive measures. The statement also ignores the fact that two days before the Declaration came out\u0026nbsp; technical evidence seems to suggest that the EU’s sanctions regime could be applied. In an \u003ca href=\"https://www.europarl.europa.eu/doceo/document/P-9-2021-001112-ASW_EN.pdf\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"answer to a parliamentary question\"\u003eanswer to a parliamentary question\u003c/a\u003e on April 13, European Commissioner for Budget and Administration Johannes Hahn indicated that the EU’s computer emergency response team (CERT-EU) had identified cases where IT networks and systems had been\u003cb\u003e “\u003c/b\u003esignificantly impacted” and personal data breaches occurred. “Significant impact” or “potentially significant effect” is one threshold \u003ca href=\"https://www.consilium.europa.eu/en/press/press-releases/2019/05/17/cyber-attacks-council-is-now-able-to-impose-sanctions/\" target=\"_blank\" title=\"covered in the sanctions regime\"\u003ecovered in the sanctions regime\u003c/a\u003e.57:[\"$\",\"$L52\",\"4\",{\"align\":\"\",\"data-block\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-start\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-end\":\"core/paragraph\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L53\",\"0\",{\"block\":{\"name\":\"core/paragraph\",\"attributes\":{\"content\":\"$64\",\"dropCap\":false}}}]]}]\n58:[\"$\",\"$L52\",\"5\",{\"align\":\"\",\"data-block\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-start\":\"core/paragraph\",\"data-block-end\":\"core/paragraph\",\"children\":[[\"$\",\"$L53\",\"0\",{\"block\":{\"name\":\"core/paragraph\",\"attributes\":{\"content\":\"Whether sanctions\u003c/a\u003e—like the United States—will also likely depend on two things: the collective determination to impose costs despite the risks of retaliation, and on the outcome of further
